**TL;DR.** I have read the brief, the four boards, the 14 references, the product data and the full source of your duplicate theme (ID 186649313493), and previewed it on a phone viewport. The theme is salvageable but needs a rebuild of the hero controller, all hero artwork, the colour system and the landing rhythm. Nothing has been executed. Two files are in your folder: [Small-Talk-Claude-Implementation-Checklist.md](Small-Talk-Claude-Implementation-Checklist.md) and [Claude-Design-Prompt.md](Claude-Design-Prompt.md).

**Where the theme actually stands**

- It is a Horizon base with Dawn files dumped on top, then a custom "desi" layer that ignores both. Roughly 800 files, most dead. Brand styling is hardcoded in one CSS file that patches itself four times over.
- The hero motion works: finite three-set autoplay, hold-to-pause, skin tones, swipe, offscreen and hidden-tab pausing, reduced motion. The clock file is clean and worth keeping.
- The hero artwork is the problem. Six PNG atlases total about 9 MB on first paint, the preview hand is the rejected five-nail version and overlaps the headline on mobile, and the character crops show rectangular edges.
- All seven products are still DRAFT at ₹0, so the theme rewrites every product link to preview templates. The real shop, PDP and cart templates are untested. Five older products (Chai Break, Gulabi Club, etc.) are still ACTIVE and priced; you need to decide whether to archive them.

**Motion code review, the short version.** Ten findings are in the checklist. The three that matter: the flying nail is a fixed-position layer on the body, so scrolling mid-flight detaches it. The take-off point is computed from a rotated element's bounding box, so the nail jumps at launch. And the nail mapping lives in two places (JS and Liquid), so they will drift. I will keep the clock and rewrite the controller.

**Colour call.** Retire red from the chrome. Red and royal blue are both high-arousal primaries; side by side they read as "sale banner", and because Diet Cherry's whole world is red, red stops carrying meaning. For this audience blue is the "do something" colour: logo, buttons, links, selected states. Product names and PDP titles go deep ink so the campaign colours own all the emotion. Plum is reserved for editorial display headings (the talks, Journal, Our Story) so those pages feel like a different room. Red lives only inside campaign art and error states. This is the brief's palette with one tweak: ink for commerce headings instead of plum.

**Model split.** I (Fable) orchestrate, own the hero controller rewrite, the colour and type system, theme cleanup, and sign off every asset. Opus 5.5 at xhigh does artwork: the four-nail hand, seven character illustrations with feathered silhouettes, chapter scenes, nail atlas re-exports. Opus 5.5 builds the landing chapters and all pages (shop, PDP, cart, journal, talks, help). Sonnet 5.5 does copy installation, SEO and AEO metadata, structured data, accessibility and performance audits. Each runs in its own session with a written brief and reports back; I merge. Order is foundation first, then artwork and motion in parallel, then pages, then SEO and QA.

**The Claude Design prompt** is below and in the file. Attach the four boards and the 14 reference images when you paste it.

````markdown
You are the art director for Small Talk, a premium press-on nail brand from India. I'm giving you four finished visualisation boards and a folder of mood references. Your job is to make a stronger v2 of all four boards: more on-brand, more on-vibe, more usable as a build reference for a Shopify store. Do not change the strategy, product names, headlines, or the page inventory. Change the craft.

## Who she is
18–24, urban, desi, independent. She grew up on Bollywood posters, Instagram collage pages, chai-and-chaos friendships, and she code-switches Hinglish without thinking. She is not a "girlboss" and hates being sold empowerment. She likes being seen as the one who already has it handled.

## What the brand is
Every nail set is named after a small moment where she naturally shows her hand, and her nails are the quiet punctuation on that moment. Seven sets, seven moments:

| Set | Headline (locked) | Note (locked) | Nail design (locked) | Colour world |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| On Me | Bill idhar. | I've got this. | Burgundy, amber tortoiseshell, gold | Mustard / amber |
| Brainwave | Haan, mera idea hai. | Called it. | Cobalt, pearly white, silver chrome | Cobalt |
| Diet Cherry | Haan, wohi wali. | My usual. | Deep red, silver chrome, blush with pointed red tips | Cherry red |
| Green Flag | Tum aa jao. Main dekh lungi. | Sorted. | Emerald, blush with pointed green tips | Emerald |
| Shaadi Shift | Dulhan tu. Duty meri. | On duty. | Pink, deep red, one yellow daisy | Fuchsia |
| Wild Card | Achha. Tum pehle. | Your move. | Acid lime, blush/lime tips, cobalt-white checker | Acid lime |
| Pinky Promise | Haath do. Main hoon. | Right here. | Lilac with deep-burgundy asymmetric pointed tips | Violet |

The moment comes first; the nails are evidence of her agency, never the cause of it. No manifesto copy, no "slay", no mascots.

## The vibe, from the references
Study the attached reference images. What they share, and what v2 must carry:
1. Desi pop-collage energy. Torn paper, Devanagari-Latin mashups, block-print and ajrakh textures as frames, stamps, scrap notes, hand-drawn hearts and crowns.
2. The hand is the hero. Henna'd hands holding a chai glass, a can, a radio, playing cards. Rings, bangles, long pointed nails. Hands doing something, never posing.
3. Vintage Bollywood poster rendering. Bold hand-lettered display type, duotone/overprint photo treatments, halftone grain, saturated flat colour fields, offset mis-registration.
4. Girls who look back at you. Retro-illustrated Indian women with winged liner, bindi, sunglasses, jhumkas, a side-eye. Attitude without aggression.
5. Warm paper, not white screen. Ivory, cream, kraft, aged newsprint under everything.

What the references do that we won't copy: real brand logos (no Coca-Cola, no Giorgio Armani), profanity in headlines, smoking, copyrighted film stills. Convert the energy, not the assets.

## What the current boards get right (keep)
- The hero structure: one fixed finger, one giant nail, handwritten note + bold Hinglish headline, next-set hand entering from top right, pause + skin-tone controls, "Shop 'Set' ↗" pill CTA.
- The campaign colour-block chapters with the nail row and "Shop set" pill.
- The stacked royal-blue "small talk" logo and the thin handwritten "the talks" in the header.
- Expressive, curved, bold display type for Hinglish headlines; handwritten notes smaller and subordinate.
- The dashed story thread linking chapters on the landing page.

## What v2 must fix
1. Colour system for chrome. Today every heading, product name and link is red beside a blue logo. Two saturated primaries fight, and red stops meaning anything because Diet Cherry's world is red. New rule: royal blue #1013B3 for the logo and every action (buttons, links, selected states). Deep ink #1F1A2E for product names and shop/PDP headings. Plum #4A243D for editorial display headings only (the talks, Journal, Our Story). Warm ivory #FFFDF5 ground, lilac tint #EEE8F5 for occasional callouts, #D8CFDA dividers. Red survives only inside campaign artwork. Show this system on the shop, PDP, bag, checkout, journal, talks and help boards.
2. More desi, less generic D2C. The current shop/PDP/help boards could be any beauty brand. Bring the collage language in as texture and framing: block-print borders on section edges, a torn-paper edge between hero and gallery, stamp-style "FEATURED" chips, kraft or newsprint behind the Journal, handwritten margin notes next to prices or sizes, a faint Devanagari watermark of the set name on each campaign chapter. Accent, not wallpaper: prices, buttons and forms stay fully readable.
3. Hero preview hand. A small relaxed hand entering from the upper right, about 20–24% of scene width, four visible nails, thumb naturally hidden, never overlapping the headline. Show it on all seven states.
4. Character crops. Half-face / eye compositions with soft, feathered silhouettes that dissolve into the background colour. No rectangular crop edges, no overlap with headline or main finger. Each woman is a distinct person; props correct: a bill, notebook with a crescent-bag sketch and blue pen, a can with no brand, a pink rotary phone, dupatta with a safety pin, cards facing her, two connected hands.
5. Landing rhythm. One campaign world should own roughly 70% of a phone screen. Right now three chapters cram into one viewport. Each chapter gets a one-line story beat, the nail row, the art, the CTA, and the thread. The thread never crosses text, nails or buttons.
6. Type hierarchy. Three voices only: bold curved display (headlines), thin handwritten (notes, "the talks"), clean readable sans (everything functional). Show sizes for 390px mobile and 1280px desktop. Body and controls legible at 16px on a phone.
7. The Talks page. Whitespace-led, conversation-first. Labelled "Example conversation", no fake reply counts, no chai icons, a private "Start a talk" form. Plum heading.
8. Journal story page. 4–6 digestible beats as a swipe carousel with a counter, correct art, a "Shop <set>" CTA at the end.
9. Shop, PDP, Bag, Checkout, Nail Guide. Functional, honest, calm. Real controls: quantity, add to bag with price, thumbnails, disclosures (What's in the kit / Fit & application / The story). Nail Guide shows paper-strip measuring, apply, remove.

## Deliverables
1. Board 1 — Hero states: all seven at 390×844 plus one desktop frame at 1280×800, plus a strip showing the four skin tones on one set and three mid-flight frames (accent flying in, accent flying back, next-set nail flying from the preview hand).
2. Board 2 — Full landing: the whole phone-length page in order: hero → vision/shop intro → Diet Cherry → the talks → Brainwave → Green Flag → Journal → Nail Guide → Shaadi Shift → On Me → Wild Card → Pinky Promise → Shop all / FAQ / footer. Plus a desktop version of the first three screens.
3. Board 3 — Read, talk, discover: the talks, a conversation thread, Journal index, Journal story, Our Story, Help.
4. Board 4 — Shopping and nail guide: Shop, Product, Bag, Checkout (Shopify checkout chrome, only colour/logo change), Nail Guide.
Plus one style sheet page: colour tokens, the three type voices with sizes, button/link/chip/input states, the collage texture kit (borders, torn edge, stamp, margin note), and a do/don't for texture density.

## Constraints
- Mobile first. Every board has a 390px version; desktop is secondary.
- Phone frames at real proportions. No artificially tall mockups.
- Use the exact nail designs listed; do not invent patterns. Nails are almond/stiletto, glossy.
- No real brand logos, no celebrities, no film stills, no Devanagari you cannot verify (ask me for exact strings; do not guess).
- Handwritten notes are always smaller than the headline.
- If something in the originals conflicts with this brief, follow this brief and flag the conflict in a note on the board.

Start with the style sheet and Board 1, show me, then do 2, 3 and 4.
````

When you say go, I start with Phase 0: full backup of the duplicate, theme cleanup, and the colour token system, then spin up the Opus artwork session and the Sonnet SEO session in parallel.

Yes, I reviewed all 14 reference images from the zip before writing the Claude Design prompt. The vibe section in that prompt is built from them: the "Desi Baddies" poster grid (Keep calm se kaam, Spill the tea girl, Tarot, Naari Shakti, Sanskaari nahi Krantikaari, Baddiegiri DJ, The Ideal Beauty, the sunglasses bride), the Diet Coke collage with the bangled hand and "Mumbai meri jaan", the "Don't stop honey" radio poster on an ajrakh frame, the Hinglish collage (देसी DIVA, कान-cept, बिच), the "बॉम्बास्टिक side eye / fine shyt / बेddie / nonचलांट" collage, the "चिल BE" duotone, the overhead card table with henna hands, the Diet कोक poster with the auto-rickshaw, the two chai-cup hands, "bitchआरी", and the orange chai-glass hand. Two cautions from them: real Diet Coke branding appears in three of them and we cannot use it, and the Diet Cherry product itself should not imply a Coca-Cola partnership, so the can in our art stays unbranded.

**Motion: how I'd build the hook**

The ChatGPT sketchboard timings are too slow for a hook. Four seconds per set with one accent is a wait, not a flick. My plan is one set every ~2.6 seconds, with real nail changes only (each set has two or three genuine designs, so that is two or three swaps, never an invented pattern).

| Local time | What moves |
|---|---|
| 0 to 0.7 s | Signature nail on the finger, headline readable |
| 0.7 to 0.95 s | Accent nail flies from the left sample onto the finger |
| 0.95 to 1.4 s | Hold |
| 1.4 to 1.65 s | Second accent (or signature return) flies in |
| 1.65 to 2.1 s | Hold |
| 2.1 to 2.6 s | Next set's signature nail flies from the upper-right hand, background cross-fades, headline and CTA commit |

Three sets autoplay then settle at about 8 seconds. Manual tap or swipe reaches all seven.

The technique, which is where the performance comes from:

- **Static first paint in Liquid.** Set one is fully laid out by CSS before any JavaScript runs. The finger image is the LCP element with high fetch priority. No layout depends on JS.
- **Compositor-only animation.** Every flight is a Web Animations API animation on `transform` and `opacity` only. No per-frame JavaScript, no layout reads during motion. Pause, hold and resume map directly to pausing the animation.
- **Flights live inside the hero.** Source and target anchors are measured in hero-local coordinates once per phase, so scrolling can never detach the nail. This fixes the two worst bugs in the current code.
- **Assets shrink from ~9 MB to ~350 KB on first paint.** One WebP sprite per set (signature, accent, extra, about 50 KB), the four finger tones as four separate files with only the selected one loaded, one hand base, and character art lazy-loaded. Set two prefetches after load, set three on idle.
- **One source of truth for nail mapping**, in the campaign metaobject, read by both Liquid and JS.
- The existing `DesiMotion` clock is kept as the state machine (pause reasons, finite pass, manual mode). Only the renderer is rewritten.

**New theme or clean the current one.** My recommendation is to start clean. The current duplicate is a Horizon base with Dawn files dumped over it and a custom layer that bypasses both. Untangling 800 files is slower and riskier than porting the 25 files that actually matter (campaign metaobject wiring, the di-* sections, nail sprites, copy) onto a fresh Horizon copy. There is already an unpublished stock Horizon theme in the store I can duplicate. Token cost for the whole project, all sessions, is roughly 3 to 5 million tokens, with artwork generation billed separately. The from-scratch path adds about 150 to 300 thousand tokens over cleanup, because I rewrite the sections rather than diff them. That is under 10 percent extra, and it removes the risk of the cleanup path blowing up when a hidden Dawn dependency breaks mid-build. These are estimates, not measurements.

**SEO and AEO ownership, updated.** I own strategy: page intent map, metadata rules, structured data design, canonical and indexing decisions, internal link architecture, the fact gates. Opus 5.5 writes every word a shopper can read: PDP stories, Journal articles, page copy, metadata titles and descriptions, alt text, FAQ answers. Sonnet is limited to technical verification: rendered HTML checks, schema validation, Lighthouse, accessibility audit. No visible copy from Sonnet.

I'll update the checklist with these three decisions.

Track A, starts now, no board dependency:

5. SEO strategy document and the Opus copy brief.

Track B, waits for Claude Design:
1. Board 1 approved → Opus artwork session starts (characters, four-nail hand, chapter scenes).
2. Boards 2 to 4 approved → landing chapter layouts, texture kit, page styling pass.

The moment you approve Board 1, artwork starts. Everything in Track A will be waiting for it. If Claude Design stalls, Track A still produces a buyable store with the current art, which is the safer outcome anyway.

### User · 2026-10-09 06:47:01

### Assistant · 2026-10-09 06:47:21

Horizon. Here is why, briefly.

Our store is about 85 percent custom: the motion hero, the seven campaign chapters, the talks, the journal story carousel, the nail guide. No off-the-shelf theme ships any of that, so whatever we pick, its own look mostly gets replaced. What we actually buy from a base theme is the boring, hard-to-get-right plumbing: product form and variant handling, cart and checkout handoff, search and filters, header and footer, drawers, accessibility, section rendering, theme editor behaviour, performance.

Horizon is the best base for that right now:

- It is Shopify's current flagship, free, and the one they are actively building on. Dawn is the previous generation.
- Its component framework, section renderer and hydration helpers are exactly what the hero controller needs to plug into cleanly. The current duplicate already carries Horizon's own authoring guide, so the patterns are documented.
- Blocks-first editor means you can reorder and hide chapters yourself without me.
- Performance baseline is the strongest of the free themes, which matters because the hero is where we spend our budget.

What I would not do:

- A paid theme (Impact, Prestige, Be Yours, around $300 to $450). We would override most of what you paid for, and their bundled JavaScript would compete with the hero for the performance budget.
- Dawn. Only worth it if Horizon's editor felt restrictive, and for our layouts it won't.

One caveat so it is not a surprise later: Horizon's stock product and collection templates look like a clean minimalist store. That is fine, because the shop, PDP and cart will be restyled to the boards anyway. We're using it as a chassis, not a look.

So: fresh Horizon duplicate. Say go.

### Assistant · 2026-10-09 07:04:02

The artwork session is deliberately split into two phases, and only the first is running now.

**Phase 1, running now, needs no design decision.** It is technical prep on assets that are already locked:

- Cutting the existing nail atlas into one small WebP sprite per set, with a documented cuticle anchor. The nail designs are final, so this is pure file engineering.
- Re-exporting the four finger tones on an exact shared canvas so the tone switch never jumps.
- Building the four-visible-nail preview hand and testing its edges on all seven campaign colours. The brief already fixed this requirement (four nails, thumb hidden, 20 to 24 percent width), and Claude Design was told to keep it, so it will not change.
- Shrinking everything from 9 MB to a ~350 KB first-paint budget.

**Phase 2, blocked until you approve Board 1.** Character illustrations, chapter scene art, texture kit. The session's brief says in capitals to wait for the orchestrator to announce Board 1 approval before touching any of it.

If Claude Design changes something in Phase 1 territory, say the hand's angle or size, the sprite and anchor system still holds and only the hand file gets redone. That is a small, cheap redo compared to having the motion controller wait on artwork.

The orchestrator reported it has confirmed the write lock with the foundation session and set up the checklist. No build work is verified yet.

### Assistant · 2026-10-09 08:06:28

The hero motion controller is built and verified locally. It is waiting on the write lock to go into the theme.

**What I built** (all in [motion/](motion/))

- [st-hero-motion.js](motion/st-hero-motion.js): the controller. Every flight is a Web Animations API animation on transform and opacity only, measured in hero-local coordinates, so scroll can never detach a nail. Finite three-set autoplay, then settle with a "Play again" state. Hold to pause by pointer or keyboard, explicit pause, hidden tab, offscreen and reduced motion are all separate pause reasons that freeze the animations in place. Tap the hand, swipe, or arrow keys to move manually. Clean teardown on theme editor section reload.
- [st-hero.css](motion/st-hero.css): the hero layout the controller depends on, with the four-nail hand slot geometry taken from the artwork session's measurements.
- [CONTRACT.md](motion/CONTRACT.md): the exact DOM hooks and JSON the Liquid section must emit.
- [lab/index.html](motion/lab/index.html): a reference page built from the artwork manifest, which is what I tested against.

**Verified in the browser at phone width.** Static first paint lands the nail on the nail bed with the four-nail hand top-right. Accent one flies in, swaps with accent two, returns. The next set's signature flies off the hand onto the finger while the scene cross-fades and the headline, note, button and preview commit. On Me moved to Brainwave to Diet Cherry as specified. Pause, hold, release on cancel, and the skin-tone cycle all behave with the right accessible labels.

**One honest gap.** I could not record the flight frames as video, because this app's preview pane stops painting when the window is behind another one, which also stalls the animation promises. Recordings will come from the installed theme preview instead.

The orchestrator has the lock request and the install plan. Once the lock is mine, I upload the script and the twelve web artwork files, rewrite the hero section to the contract on top of the foundation session's latest version, and verify at 320, 390, 430 and 1280 in the real theme preview.

No. I checked the actual product records a moment ago: every one of the seven products has exactly one media item, the packaging PNG. That is all ChatGPT ever uploaded.

The four or five thumbnails you saw on its board and in its preview came from two places. The board was a visualisation, not the store. The controller now handles the per-set characters too, and the hand-off is complete.

What changed in this round:

- Characters commit with each set: fade out with the flight, swap, fade in, anchored to the hero corner the artwork session measured for each woman. The next set's character is decoded during the hold so the swap never stalls, and sets two to seven are never fetched at first paint.
- When a character owns the top-left corner (Brainwave, Pinky Promise), the headline moves right so it never crosses her face. Verified at 390 and 1280 against board 2.
- The Shop button follows a per-set live flag, so draft sets never look purchasable, and the nail's screen-reader description updates on every commit.

The orchestrator has the final file hashes and the exact Liquid additions. The foundation session installs the Liquid side and copies my two files in one batch, then I run the final verification on all seven states, four tones and four widths on the real preview.

### Assistant · 2026-10-09 09:30:38

The orchestrator confirmed the seven products are live and correct. It also confirmed the policies are still missing, as expected, because the connector can't write them. Its readback found three more settings that only you can change in Shopify admin. Checkout won't work until the first one is done.

1. **No payment provider (blocks checkout).** Checkout currently says "This store can't accept payments right now." To fix it, go to Settings → Payments and activate a provider. Razorpay, PayU, Cashfree or PhonePe all work for India. Each one asks for your business and bank details, so it has to be you.
2. **Policies.** Paste the two texts from [policies/Shipping-and-Returns-Policies.md](policies/Shipping-and-Returns-Policies.md) into Settings → Policies.
3. **Price shows as "Rs. 599.00".** To show ₹599 instead, go to Settings → General → Store currency → Change formatting. Set both "HTML with currency" and "HTML without currency" to `₹{{amount_no_decimals}}`, and do the same for the two email formats.
4. **Shipping rate is named "मानक".** Customers see this name at checkout. Go to Settings → Shipping and delivery → General shipping rates and edit the domestic rate. Rename it to something like "Standard delivery (up to 7 days)", and check the price is what you want to charge.

Tell me when these are done. The orchestrator will then read the settings back and run a full test of the bag and checkout. It won't place a real order.

Nothing in this setup can paint new illustrations: Claude can't, and the one generator connected, Canva, isn't signed in.

What exists at full size and matches the boards:

What doesn't exist as separate files:

- The chapter props: the notebook with the crescent-bag sketch and pen, the pink rotary phone, the saree with the safety pin, the hand holding cards.
- The tight half-face crops peeking in from the edges in the poses shown.

These have to come from ChatGPT, where the boards were made. I can write one exact prompt per missing piece, each asking for a transparent background, so they drop straight in.

- **Chapter layout:** it became a split panel. The boards have a centred headline, a row of five big nails, a white pill button, art bleeding in from the bottom, and torn wavy edges between sections.
- **Hero:** the main nail is too small. On the board it fills about 45% of the width. The headline is too light and not tilted enough, and the Diet Cherry can is missing.
- **Colours:** I put ink and plum colours on headings and buttons. The boards use red and white pills and their own colours, so the boards win.
- **Fonts:** the board lettering was drawn by the AI and isn't a real font. The closest licensed fonts get very close but not pixel-identical. The headline needs a much heavier serif than the one we use now.
- **Process:** the sessions checked their work against the written brief and accessibility rules instead of laying it over each board frame. That is how the drift happened.

**Realistic outcome:** once the missing props come from ChatGPT and we rebuild to the boards, the store matches them closely: the same characters, props, layout, sizes, colours, glows and wavy edges. It records positions and sizes, exact colours sampled from the boards, glow gradients, and the curve of each wavy section edge. ### Assistant · 2026-10-10 05:49:38

The full prompt pack is ready in [artwork/ChatGPT-Artwork-Prompts.md](artwork/ChatGPT-Artwork-Prompts.md). I also built a reference folder for each set in `artwork/chatgpt-refs/`, with the exact board crop, the hero frame, the woman, the nail designs and the box. That way ChatGPT copies your boards instead of guessing.

**Why it's blurry now and how this fixes it**

- All seven women came out of a single ChatGPT image, so each is only about 300 px wide and gets stretched on phones. The nails and finger are also 2 to 3 times too small for how big the hero shows them.
- The pack asks for one subject per image, at ChatGPT's largest size, with a transparent background.
- Always download the original PNG from ChatGPT. Never screenshot it or send it through WhatsApp, which compresses it again.

**How to run it**

1. Open one ChatGPT chat per set, plus one shared chat. Staying in one chat per set keeps each woman and her nails consistent.
2. In each set's chat, attach the five files from `artwork/chatgpt-refs/<set>/` plus `_shared/character-sheet.png`.
3. Paste this first:

```text
I'm making artwork for my press-on nail brand's website. The attached board images are the approved design; match them exactly. File 1 is a section of the home page, file 2 is the hero screen, file 3 shows the woman for this set, file 4 shows the exact nail designs (left to right: signature nail, accent nail, extra accent), file 5 is the product box, and the character sheet shows the illustration style.

For every illustration in this chat: bold comic-ink Indian pop illustration exactly like the boards — thick confident black ink outlines, flat saturated colour with soft cel shading, glossy highlights, winged eyeliner, bindi, gold jewellery, dense curly black hair. Hands have exactly five fingers with natural joints. Nails are long almond press-ons, glossy, matching file 4 exactly in colour and pattern.

For every realistic photo in this chat: high-end beauty product photography, soft studio light, crisp focus on the nails, natural medium-brown Indian skin with real texture.

Always: one subject per image, filling the frame, the largest image size you can make, PNG. No text, no logos, no watermark, no border, unless I ask for specific text. When I say "cutout", the background must be transparent.

Reply "ready" and wait for my first request.
```

4. Then paste that set's prompts from the file one by one. Save each result to `artwork/chatgpt/<set>/` with the file name given.

**What you get, in priority order**

- **P1, fixes the home page:**
  - the seven women
  - the board props: receipt hand, sketchbook, can hand, pink rotary phone, dupatta with safety pin, cards hand
  - every nail as its own HD render
  - the four finger tones
  - the Our Vision tile
- **P2, product galleries:** three photos per set (nail fan on the set colour, a hand wearing the set, a hand doing the set's moment) plus three Nail Guide photos.
- **P3, optional:** a high-resolution re-render of each box. Use one only if the text and illustration come out exactly as printed.

Check every image against the nail table at the bottom of the file, because ChatGPT drifts on nail patterns. Make sure the nail-fan photos don't show a different number of nails than your real kit.

The orchestrator knows the file names. As each image lands, the artwork session cleans and sizes it, the foundation session swaps it in, and Fable re-checks that screen against the board.

### Assistant · 2026-10-10 06:21:33

I've started a dedicated session for the talks: [Small Talk — the talks: real anonymous threads](#local_2c74a7fd-f54e-43a8-9df1-0baf05c20c22).

**Why your talk didn't appear.** The current "Start a talk" box is only a contact form. It emails the team, and nothing gets published. Replies run on Shopify's blog comments, which always demand a name and email. A Shopify theme on its own can't publish what a shopper writes, so the feature needs a small backend.

**What the new session builds:**

- Reddit-style threads in the board's look. She picks a topic or types her own, writes inside the thread, taps once, and it appears straight away. Replies work the same way.
- No email, no name, no login. Each phone gets a playful auto-alias she can change.
- Talks are stored in your Shopify admin, so you can hide or delete any of them there.
- Quiet safety for an anonymous gossip space. Posts with phone numbers, emails, @handles or slurs are blocked. Posts that seem to target a real person are held for review. Every post has a report link and hides itself after 3 reports, and there's a posting limit to stop spam.

**One step will need you.** Connecting the backend to your store needs a secret key that only the store owner should create and paste. The session will give you exact click-by-click steps when it reaches that point.

**Login.** Shopify already has customer accounts built in. Customers sign in with a one-time code sent to their email (no password), or with "Sign in with Shop". Their orders and addresses are saved and checkout fills itself in. You can check this under Settings → Customer accounts.

**Sign in with Google isn't available on your plan.** Shopify's own Google or custom login connection is only on Shopify Plus. Third-party social-login apps exist, but they cost money and use Shopify's older account system. It isn't worth it at launch.

**My recommendation:**

- Keep Shopify's code login, and add a "Sign in / My orders" link inside the menu drawer. Your board's header stays exactly as it is.
- Keep the talks anonymous with no login, since that's the whole appeal. Later, signed-in customers could keep the same alias across devices.

Say yes and the sign-in link goes into the menu.

### Assistant · 2026-10-10 08:15:18

I'd advise against this one. It wouldn't get you a messaging funnel, and it would break the promise the talks make.

**Why capturing IP addresses doesn't work for this:**

- **You can't message an IP address.** It isn't a phone number or an email. It's often shared by a whole household or office, and on mobile data it changes constantly.
- **We can't see it anyway.** Posts travel through Shopify, so the backend only ever sees Shopify's own address, never the shopper's. Collecting real addresses would mean taking posting off Shopify's protected route.
- **It contradicts what the page tells her.** The talks are sold as anonymous, and the page tells her exactly what's kept. Quietly collecting more breaks that promise. India's data protection law also requires telling people what you collect and why.

**What actually builds a messaging funnel from someone's first visit:**

1. **Ad retargeting.** Add the Meta and Google tracking pixels in Shopify (Settings → Customer events, plus the Facebook & Instagram app). Everyone who views the store or the talks, and accepts the cookie banner, goes into an audience you can show Instagram and Meta ads to. This is the standard first-visit funnel, and it doesn't touch the talks' anonymity.
2. **"Get told when someone replies."** After she posts, show an optional field: "Want a ping when someone replies? Add your WhatsApp or email." It costs her nothing to post, people opt in because they want the replies, and it gives you a real contact with consent for messaging. This asks for contact details only after posting and only if she wants, so it respects your "no email yet" rule for the posting step itself.
3. **Quietly linking signed-in customers.** If she's signed in to your store, the backend can privately record which customer wrote a post, visible only to you in admin and never on the site. You can then message her through Shopify's own email marketing, if she has agreed to marketing.
4. **A signup offer on the store.** Shopify Forms (free) can show a "10% off your first set" WhatsApp or email signup.

My recommendation is all four: the pixels and Shopify Forms now (you set those up in admin), and options 2 and 3 built into the talks by the talks session. The privacy text then gets updated to say exactly what's kept.

Tell me which of these you want and I'll send them out, and I'll write the pixel setup steps for you.

```text
Riya
```

| Section | She keeps | Moves to our list |
|---|---|---|

| Check the facts | One task: "Confirm 6 facts about your real kit", plus real product photos (optional, later) | Sending the corrections to Claude |
| Turn on the talks | Verify her email, paste the keys in Vercel, the 2-minute daily moderation check | Creating, configuring and installing the app (I do it in Chrome), the v0 Publish, the privacy paragraph, retiring the old talks |
| Go live | Walk the preview on her phone, then publish the store herself, with rollback folded in | Test files, old pages and menus, password check, removing unused apps and pixels |
| Know your visitors | Meta business account and Instagram pro, connect Meta to the store and check events, Google & YouTube app + GA4, Search Console + sitemap, a 10-minute Shopify Analytics tour | Cookie banner, domain decision |
| Retargeting ads | When to start, building audiences, a first small campaign and a weekly check (5 tasks merged into 3) | Nothing |
| UTM links | One task: make and track a link with the builder | Nothing |
| Email & WhatsApp | Welcome discount + welcome email, choosing a WhatsApp setup | Shopify Forms install, checkout consent setting |
| Talks upgrades | Nothing. She only sees "coming soon" | All of it |
| Instagram | Learn the voice, set up the carousel chat, make a post | Nothing |
| AI videos | The same workflow, merged from 8 tasks into 5, with the worked example kept | Nothing |

**2. Add the missing Instagram strategy.** You asked for content building and maintenance, and right now there's only a posting-rhythm task. I'd add two:

- "Set up your profile": bio, the link in bio with tracking, highlights and pinned posts.

**3. Mark tasks clearly on the collapsed bar:**

- ✨ **Prompt inside**: a copy-paste AI prompt she uses herself.
- 🔒 **Only you**: anything involving her identity, money or keys.
- ⏱ the time it takes.

Messages addressed to Claude disappear from her tab, because those tasks become ours.

**4. Training wheels.** A pinned "Stuck? Copy this" helper at the top. It's one prompt that gives ChatGPT or Claude the store's context, so she can ask anything and get an answer that fits her store.

**5. A sensible "Do this next" order:** payments, test order, talks keys, preview, publish, Meta, Google, the Instagram profile, carousels, AI video, UTM, email, then retargeting once real visitors exist.

**One permission I'd need from you.** Several of the moved tasks are changes to settings in Riya's Shopify admin, such as the policy pastes, ₹ format, shipping name, SEO title, logo, menus and apps. <h2>the talks (our community board)</h2>
```
<p>You can post on the talks without a name, email address or account. What you post is public on our site: your talk or reply, the alias you chose, your avatar and the time you posted. Your alias, avatar, a random device ID and any unsent draft are saved only in your browser’s storage, and clearing your browser data removes them. To prevent spam and abuse, each time you post, reply or report, we keep a short record made from a scrambled, one-way version of that device ID, plus a scrambled version of the text so we can catch repeats. Posts are stored in our Shopify store, and our posting service runs on Vercel, a hosting provider; both process each request only to deliver it. ### Assistant · 2026-10-10 12:58:22
```

Everything is filled in and waiting in your Chrome. There are 8 tabs, left to right, and each one just needs **Save** (top right, or at the bottom of a popup):

| # | Tab | What I filled in |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Policies → Refund | 15-day returns policy |
| 2 | Policies → Terms of service | Full terms, including the talks rules |
| 3 | Policies → Shipping | India only, dispatch in 2 business days, delivery in up to 7 days (address-change section removed) |
| 4 | Policies → Privacy | Shopify's text, plus a new "the talks" section |
| 5 | General → currency formatting | ₹499 instead of "Rs. 499.00" |
| 6 | Shipping → General profile | Rate renamed "मानक" → "Standard delivery" (₹379 unchanged) |
| 7 | Online Store → Preferences | New homepage title and description (both say "Almond" now; the new ones don't) |
| 8 | Checkout editor | Small Talk logo at the top of checkout, left-aligned |

**Three things to know:**
- **Privacy (tab 4):** to add the talks section I had to switch off "Use automated policy". After that, Shopify stops updating this policy by itself, for example when Riya connects the Meta pixel. If you'd rather keep the automatic version, click Cancel on that tab; the talks section just won't be in the policy.

### Assistant · 2026-10-10 17:59:14

The hero stops after three sets because that limit was built in on purpose: the original motion plan capped autoplay so it wouldn't run forever. I've asked the design session to change it to what you described:

- **Loop:** it cycles through all seven sets and keeps going.
- **Pause:** tap anywhere on the hero art, or press the pause button. Taps on the Shop button and the skin-tone circle still do their own job, and once someone pauses, it stays paused.
- **Saving battery:** it pauses itself while scrolled out of view or when the browser tab is in the background, then carries on unless the visitor paused it.
- **Reduced motion:** phones set to "reduce motion" still start paused, as now.

This applies on phone and laptop and goes into the same publish as the chapter layouts, dotted line, Wild Card cards and Journal fix. I've also asked for a 10-minute test so the endless loop doesn't slow the page down.

The Wild Card ace and king are also done: vector cards, a black ace of spades and a red king of hearts, coloured to match the existing card art. The design session approved them, so you don't need to make them in ChatGPT.

- **Links:** tapping a row jumps to that task's card, opens it and briefly highlights it, even if the card is in another section.

It'll be republished at the same link with her ticks kept and tested at phone width. I'll pass on the summary when it's done.

For the talks setup, I'm still waiting for your "go" to create and set up the app in your Chrome.

I created and set up the talks app in your Dev Dashboard, and its install screen is open in your Chrome.

**What's set up:**
- An app called **Small Talk talks** with only the three permissions it needs: read and write the talks entries, plus the app proxy.
- The storefront link `/apps/talks` now points to our server. That's version 2, and it's active.
- It doesn't show inside your Shopify admin.

**Your steps now:**
1. **Install:** in the open tab "Small Talk · Install app", click **Install**. The screen shows two permissions: viewing the store owner's details, which every app gets, and editing "Online Store, custom data", which covers the talks entries and the `/apps/talks` link. The "hasn't been reviewed" warning is normal for a private app you made yourself.
2. **Copy the Client ID and Client secret** from the Dev Dashboard tab (app overview → Credentials) **straight into Vercel**, along with the other two values. The table from my earlier message lists all four; mark the secret and `TALKS_SALT` as Sensitive. Don't paste them in chat.
3. **Publish in v0:** open the chat **small-talk-talks-backend** in v0.app and click **Publish**, not Vercel's Redeploy.
4. **Tell me "talks app installed".** I'll confirm the server shows version `c91d3d11afc1` and "configured". The talks session then runs the full test and deletes its test posts.

**Your design direction for the talks** has gone to the design, talks and copy sessions in your words: simple, structured and minimal, like a Reddit thread for college girls, inviting and friendly, with gossip and "tea" energy. That means:
- **Layout:** a clear topic list, and threads showing nickname, avatar, time and reply count, with replies nested underneath.
- **Posting:** a one-tap "spill your tea" box with short prompts to get started.
- **Text:** catchy fonts only for headings, readable text for posts, nothing oversized, and a casual lowercase voice.
- **Kept as built:** anonymous, no login, and no IP addresses stored.

You'll see before-and-after screenshots on phone and laptop before anything is installed, and it'll be ready around when posting is switched on.

### Assistant · 2026-10-10 19:42:24

I've added dependencies to Riya's guide, so nothing shows as a priority before the tasks it depends on are done. It's queued behind the priority update the guide session is working on now.

**How it works:**
- **Ready vs waiting:** each task lists what it needs first, and only counts as ready once all of those are ticked.
- **"Do this next":** it always shows the highest-priority task that's ready, never a blocked one.
- **Blocked tasks** stay in their priority group, faded, with a ⏳ "waiting on: …" line that links to the task she needs to do first.
- **Prerequisites move up:** if a high-priority task depends on a lower one, the lower one is listed just before it, marked "needed first for …".
- **Time waits** show a date. For example, retargeting ads show "ready from <date>", at least 2 weeks after she confirms her Meta tracking is working.
- **Outside waits,** like "talks posting is open", clear on their own once that happens.

**Main dependencies I set:**
- **Store:** test order ← payments; publish ← preview + test order + kit facts confirmed.
- **Meta:** business portfolio → Instagram professional → Facebook & Instagram app → check events (which also needs the store published).
- **Google:** GA4 and Search Console ← store published (the verification tag has to be on the live store).
- **Instagram:** profile tune-up → content mix → weekly batch day and monthly calendar → weekly review.
- **Carousels:** learn the voice → set up → make ideas.
- **AI video:** pick tools → character sheet and script → storyboard → animate and post.
- **Email:** welcome discount → signup form → welcome email.
- **Ads:** audiences ← events working + 2 weeks of data; first campaign ← audiences + ad copy; weekly ad check ← first campaign.

I've asked the guide session to send me the full table and flag any task that could end up blocked forever, so I can check it before you share the guide with Riya.
